# Taylor Swift

This article is about the singer-songwriter. For other uses, see [Taylor Swift (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_\(disambiguation\)).

**Taylor Alison Swift** (born December 13, 1989) is an American singer-songwriter. [An influential figure in popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Taylor_Swift), Swift is known for her autobiographical songwriting and artistic reinventions. She is the [highest-grossing live music artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_live_music_artists), the [wealthiest female musician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_music_artists_by_net_worth), and one of the [best-selling music artists of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best-selling_music_artists_of_all_time).

Swift signed with [Big Machine Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Machine_Records) in 2005 and debuted as a [country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music) singer with the albums *[Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_\(album\))* (2006) and *[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_\(Taylor_Swift_album\))* (2008). The singles "[Teardrops on My Guitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teardrops_on_My_Guitar)", "[Love Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Story_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", and "[You Belong with Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Belong_with_Me)" found [crossover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crossover_music) success on [country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_radio) and [pop radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_radio) formats. Her songs began incorporating stronger [rock](25423.md) elements with *[Speak Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now)* (2010) and [electronic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music) styles with *[Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_\(Taylor_Swift_album\))* (2012). She subsequently recalibrated her artistic identity from country to [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music) with the [synth-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synth-pop) album *[1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_\(album\))* (2014), while ensuing media scrutiny inspired the [trap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trap_music)-imbued *[Reputation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_\(album\))* (2017). Through the 2010s, Swift accumulated the US [*Billboard* Hot 100](423161.md) number-one singles "[We Are Never Ever Getting Back Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Are_Never_Ever_Getting_Back_Together)", "[Shake It Off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shake_It_Off)", "[Blank Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blank_Space)", "[Bad Blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Blood_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", and "[Look What You Made Me Do](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do)".

Shifting to [Republic Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Records) in 2018, Swift released the [eclectic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclecticism_in_music) pop album *[Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_\(album\))* (2019) and [re-recorded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-recorded) four of her first six albums due to [a dispute with Big Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_masters_dispute). She explored [indie folk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indie_folk) on the 2020 albums *[Folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_\(Taylor_Swift_album\))* and *[Evermore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evermore)*, followed by mellower pop subgenres on *[Midnights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnights)* (2022) and *[The Tortured Poets Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tortured_Poets_Department)* (2024), as well as [soft rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_rock) on *[The Life of a Showgirl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_a_Showgirl)* (2025). The singles "[Cardigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardigan_\(song\))", "[Willow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_\(song\))", "[All Too Well (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Too_Well_\(Taylor's_Version\))", "[Anti-Hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Hero_\(song\))", "[Cruel Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", "[Is It Over Now?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Is_It_Over_Now%3F)", "[Fortnight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortnight_\(song\))", "[The Fate of Ophelia](81126617.md)", "[Opalite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opalite_\(song\))", and "[I Knew It, I Knew You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Knew_It,_I_Knew_You)" topped the Hot 100. Her [Eras Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eras_Tour) (2023–2024) and its associated film, *[Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Eras_Tour)* (2023), are the [highest-grossing concert tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours) and [concert film of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concert_film#Highest-grossing_concert_films).

Swift is the only artist to have been named the [IFPI Global Recording Artist of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFPI_Global_Recording_Artist_of_the_Year) six times. A record eight of her albums have each sold over a million copies first-week in the US. Publications such as *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)* and [*Billboard*](18309966.md) have ranked Swift among the greatest artists of all time. She is the first individual from [the arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_arts) to be named [*Time* Person of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Person_of_the_Year) (2023) and the youngest female inductee into the [Songwriters Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Songwriters_Hall_of_Fame) (2026). [Her accolades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Taylor_Swift) include 14 [Grammy Awards](44635.md)—including a record four [Album of the Year](196513.md) wins—and a [Primetime Emmy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award). Swift is the most-awarded artist of the [American Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Music_Awards), the [*Billboard* Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_Music_Awards), and the [MTV Video Music Awards](295217.md). A subject of [extensive media coverage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_image_of_Taylor_Swift), she has a global fanbase known as [Swifties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swifties).

## Life and career

### Early life

Taylor Alison Swift was born on December 13, 1989, in [West Reading, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Reading,_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-1] She is named after the singer-songwriter [James Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Taylor);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone20173-2] her parents chose a unisex name, hoping it would help her succeed in business.[^cite_note-Grigoriadis-2009-3] Her father, Scott Kingsley Swift, was a [stockbroker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockbroker) for [Merrill Lynch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrill_Lynch), and her mother, Andrea Gardner Swift ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) Finlay), worked as a [mutual fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_fund) marketing executive.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJepson20131-4] Swift's younger brother, [Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Swift), is an actor who manages her film projects.[^cite_note-5] Taylor and Austin are of Scottish, English, and German descent, with distant Irish and Italian ancestry; they are paternal great-great-grandchildren of [Charles Carmine Antonio Baldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Carmine_Antonio_Baldi), a prominent Philadelphia businessman.[^cite_note-9] Their maternal grandmother, [Marjorie Finlay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Finlay) ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) Moehlenkamp), was an opera singer whose singing in church became one of Swift's earliest memories of music.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer20104-10]

During childhood, Swift spent her [holiday seasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiday_season) on a [Christmas tree farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_tree_farm) in Pennsylvania,[^cite_note-11] and summers at her family's vacation home in [Stone Harbor, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Harbor,_New_Jersey), where she occasionally performed acoustic songs at a local coffee shop.[^cite_note-12] Raised Christian,[^cite_note-13] she attended preschool and kindergarten at a [Montessori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montessori) school run by the [Bernardine Sisters of St. Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardine_Sisters_of_St._Francis) before transferring to the Wyndcroft School in [Pottstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pottstown,_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-14][^cite_note-15] When her family moved to [Wyomissing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyomissing), she attended [Wyomissing Area Junior/Senior High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyomissing_Area_Junior/Senior_High_School).[^cite_note-16][^cite_note-17] At age nine, she aspired to a career in [musical theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_theater), performing at local festivals and in Berks Youth Theatre Academy productions,[^cite_note-18][^cite_note-Rolling_Stone_Interview-19] and traveling regularly to New York City for vocal and acting lessons.[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarlin201914-21] After watching a documentary about [Faith Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faith_Hill), she changed her goal and became determined to pursue a [country music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music) career in [Nashville, Tennessee](22018.md).[^cite_note-22]

At the age of 11, Swift traveled to Nashville with her mother to visit record labels and submit [demo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demo_\(music\)) tapes of [Dolly Parton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolly_Parton) and [Dixie Chicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dixie_Chicks) karaoke covers.[^cite_note-23] She was rejected by all the labels, which led her to focus on songwriting.[^cite_note-24] She started learning the guitar at the age of 12 with the help of a computer repairman and local musician who assisted Swift in writing an original song.[^cite_note-25] In 2003, she and her parents started working with the talent manager Dan Dymtrow. With his help, Swift modeled for [Abercrombie & Fitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abercrombie_&_Fitch), had an original song included on a [Maybelline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maybelline) compilation CD, and was given an [artist development deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artist_development_deal) from [RCA Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCA_Records) at 13.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer201010-27] To help Swift break into the country music scene, her father transferred to Merrill Lynch's Nashville office when she was 14 years old, and the family relocated to [Hendersonville, Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendersonville,_Tennessee).[^cite_note-Widdicombe-2011-28][^cite_note-Jo_Sales-2013-29] Swift attended [Hendersonville High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendersonville_High_School_\(Tennessee\)) for two years before transferring to Aaron Academy, which offered homeschooling.[^cite_note-Grigoriadis-2009-3][^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31]

### 2004–2008: Career beginnings and *Taylor Swift*

Taylor Swift speaking into a mic, wearing a white sundress while holding her framed album

Swift promoting her debut album in 2007

Swift signed with [Sony/ATV Tree Music Publishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony/ATV_Music_Publishing) in 2004; at 14, she became the youngest person in that publishing company's history.[^cite_note-32] In Nashville, she worked with experienced [Music Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_Row) songwriters, including [Liz Rose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liz_Rose).[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] Rose and Swift would write songs every Tuesday afternoon after school.[^cite_note-35] After one year on the development deal, she left RCA Records, which was planning to keep her in development until she turned 18.[^cite_note-36] Swift made this decision because she wanted to release the songs immediately to ensure that they still resonated with her teenage experiences.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer201012-37]

Swift organized a showcase concert at the [Bluebird Cafe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluebird_Cafe) on November 3, 2004; among the attendees was [Scott Borchetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Borchetta), a music executive who was planning to establish an [independent record label](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_record_label) called [Big Machine Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Machine_Records).[^cite_note-38] She signed a [recording contract](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recording_contract) with Big Machine two weeks after the concert, on the condition that her albums would be self-written;[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer201021-40] her father purchased a three-percent stake in the company.[^cite_note-41] The contract was finalized by July 2005, when Swift ended her working relationship with Dymtrow.[^cite_note-42] She spent four months near the end of 2005 recording her debut album, [*Taylor Swift*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_\(album\)), with the producer [Nathan Chapman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathan_Chapman_\(record_producer\)).[^cite_note-Morris-2006-43]

Swift's debut single, "[Tim McGraw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_McGraw_\(song\))", was released in June 2006. She and her mother spent mid-2006 sending promotional copies of the song to [country radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_radio) stations across the US.[^cite_note-44] *Taylor Swift* was released on October 24, 2006.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer201029-45] On the US [*Billboard* 200](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_200) chart, the album peaked at number five and spent 157 weeks on the chart—the longest chart run by an album in the 2000s.[^cite_note-46] With *Taylor Swift*, she became the first female country music artist to write or co-write every track on a [platinum-certified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum-certified) debut album.[^cite_note-47] Swift promoted the album with a six-month radio tour and by [opening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opening_act) for other country artists, including [Rascal Flatts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rascal_Flatts) in 2006,[^cite_note-Morris-2006-43][^cite_note-48] and [George Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Strait),[^cite_note-49] [Brad Paisley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Paisley),[^cite_note-50] and [Tim McGraw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_McGraw) and Faith Hill in 2007.[^cite_note-51] She opened for Rascal Flatts again in 2008,[^cite_note-52] when she dated the singer [Joe Jonas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Jonas).[^cite_note-53]

*Taylor Swift* was supported by four more singles in 2007 and 2008: "[Teardrops on My Guitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teardrops_on_My_Guitar)", "[Our Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Song)", "[Picture to Burn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picture_to_Burn)", and "[Should've Said No](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Should've_Said_No)". "Our Song" and "Should've Said No" reached number one on the [Hot Country Songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_Country_Songs) chart; with the former single, Swift became the youngest person to single-handedly write and sing a number-one country single.[^cite_note-54] "Teardrops on My Guitar" was Swift's breakthrough single on mainstream radio and charts, reaching the top 10 of the [Pop Songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_Songs), [Adult Pop Songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_Pop_Songs), and [Adult Contemporary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_Contemporary_\(chart\)) charts.[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56] Her next releases were the [Christmas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_music) [extended play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extended_play) (EP) *[The Taylor Swift Holiday Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Taylor_Swift_Holiday_Collection)* in October 2007,[^cite_note-57] and the [Walmart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walmart)-exclusive EP *[Beautiful Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beautiful_Eyes)* in July 2008.[^cite_note-58] Swift became the youngest person to be awarded with the [Nashville Songwriters Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_Songwriters_Association_International)'s Songwriter/Artist of the Year in 2007.[^cite_note-59]

### 2008–2010: *Fearless*

Taylor Swift wearing a dark strapless dress with her hair pinned up

Swift at the 2009 premiere of *[Hannah Montana: The Movie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannah_Montana:_The_Movie)*. She had a cameo appearance in the film and wrote two songs for [its soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannah_Montana:_The_Movie_\(soundtrack\)).

Swift's second album, *[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_\(Taylor_Swift_album\))*, was released on November 11, 2008, in North America,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer201051-60] and in March 2009 in other markets.[^cite_note-61] *Fearless* spent eleven weeks at number one on the *Billboard* 200, becoming her first chart topping album and the longest-running number-one female country album; it was the best-selling album of 2009 in the US.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63] The album's lead single, "[Love Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Story_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", became the first country song to top the Pop Songs chart, and its third single, "[You Belong with Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Belong_with_Me)", was the first country song to top *[Billboard](18309966.md)*'s all-genre [Radio Songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Songs_\(chart\)) chart;[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] both reached the top five of the [*Billboard* Hot 100](423161.md) chart and peaked atop the Hot Country Songs chart.[^cite_note-Billboard-3-66][^cite_note-Billboard-2-67] Three other singles—"[White Horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Horse_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", "[Fifteen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifteen_\(song\))", "[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))"—all reached the top 10 of Hot Country Songs.[^cite_note-Billboard-2-67] In 2009, Swift opened for [Keith Urban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Urban)'s tour and embarked on her first headlining concert tour, the [Fearless Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_Tour) (2009–2010).[^cite_note-68]

*Fearless* became the most-awarded country album of all time.[^cite_note-69] It won the three highest awards for a country album: Album of the Year by both the [Country Music Association Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Music_Association_Awards) and [Academy of Country Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Country_Music_Awards) in 2009, and [Best Country Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Country_Album) by the [Grammy Awards](44635.md) in 2010.[^cite_note-70] At the Grammy Awards, *Fearless* also won [Album of the Year](196513.md), and "White Horse" won [Best Country Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Country_Song) and [Best Female Country Vocal Performance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Female_Country_Vocal_Performance).[^cite_note-71] Also in 2009, Swift was named Artist of the Year by both the [American Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Music_Awards_of_2009) and *Billboard*,[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] and Entertainer of the Year by the Country Music Association Awards, becoming the youngest person to receive the honor.[^cite_note-74] "You Belong with Me" won [Best Female Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Female_Video) at the [MTV Video Music Awards](295217.md). Her acceptance speech was interrupted by the rapper [Kanye West](523032.md), [an incident that became the subject of controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_MTV_Video_Music_Awards#Kanye_West%E2%80%93Taylor_Swift_incident) and widespread media coverage.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECullen201633-75]

Swift featured in 2009 on "[Half of My Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Half_of_My_Heart)" by [John Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Mayer), with whom she was romantically linked later that year.[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-Billboard-2014-77] She wrote "[Best Days of Your Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Days_of_Your_Life)" for [Kellie Pickler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kellie_Pickler),[^cite_note-78] co-wrote and featured on [Boys Like Girls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boys_Like_Girls)' "[Two Is Better Than One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Is_Better_Than_One),[^cite_note-79] and wrote "[You'll Always Find Your Way Back Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You'll_Always_Find_Your_Way_Back_Home)" and wrote and recorded "[Crazier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crazier)" for the [soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannah_Montana:_The_Movie_\(soundtrack\)) of *[Hannah Montana: The Movie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannah_Montana:_The_Movie)*, in which she also had a cameo appearance.[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81] She made her acting debut in the 2010 rom-com *[Valentine's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentine's_Day_\(2010_film\))* and wrote "[Today Was a Fairytale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Today_Was_a_Fairytale)" for [its soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentine's_Day_\(soundtrack\)).[^cite_note-82] "Today Was a Fairytale" reached number one on the [Canadian Hot 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Hot_100).[^cite_note-83] While shooting *Valentine's Day* in October 2009, Swift dated co-star [Taylor Lautner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Lautner).[^cite_note-84] She made her television debut as a rebellious teenager in a *[CSI: Crime Scene Investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSI:_Crime_Scene_Investigation)* episode[^cite_note-85] and hosted and performed as the musical guest on *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live)*; she was the first host to write their own opening monologue.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87]

### 2010–2014: *Speak Now* and *Red*

Taylor Swift singing into a mic while playing a guitar, dressed in a purple dress

Swift on the [Speak Now World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_World_Tour) in 2011

Swift wrote her third album, *[Speak Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now)*, entirely by herself.[^cite_note-Caramanica-2010-88] Released on October 25, 2010,[^cite_note-89] it debuted atop the US *Billboard* 200 with over one million first-week copies sold—the highest single-week tally for a female country artist.[^cite_note-90] Five of its singles—"[Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mine_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", "[Back to December](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_to_December)", "[Mean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mean_\(song\))", "[Sparks Fly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparks_Fly_\(song\))", and "[Ours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ours_\(song\))"—charted in the top three of Hot Country Songs; "Sparks Fly" and "Ours" reached number one.[^cite_note-Billboard-2-67] "Mine" peaked at number three and was the album's highest-charting single on the *Billboard* Hot 100,[^cite_note-91] and "Mean" became its best-selling track.[^cite_note-92] Swift supported the album with the [Speak Now World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_World_Tour) (2011–2012) and its accompanying live album, *[Speak Now World Tour – Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_World_Tour_%E2%80%93_Live)* (2011).[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94]

In 2011, Swift was honored as Woman of the Year by *Billboard*,[^cite_note-Roland-2011-95] Entertainer of the Year by both the Academy of Country Music Awards and the Country Music Association Awards,[^cite_note-96] and Artist of the Year at the American Music Awards.[^cite_note-97] She again won Entertainer of the Year at the Academy of Country Music Awards in 2012.[^cite_note-98] At the [54th Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/54th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) in 2012, "Mean" won Best Country Song and [Best Country Solo Performance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Country_Solo_Performance).[^cite_note-99] After the release of *Speak Now*, Swift briefly dated the actor [Jake Gyllenhaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Gyllenhaal).[^cite_note-Billboard-2014-77]

On October 22, 2012, Swift released her fourth album, *[Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_\(Taylor_Swift_album\))*, which she produced with Chapman and several new collaborators, including [Max Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Martin), [Shellback](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shellback_\(record_producer\)), [Dan Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Wilson_\(musician\)), [Jeff Bhasker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Bhasker), [Dann Huff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dann_Huff), and [Butch Walker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butch_Walker).[^cite_note-Lewis-2012-100][^cite_note-Mansfield-2012-101] It debuted at number one on the *Billboard* 200 with 1.21 million sales, becoming the fastest-selling country album in US history.[^cite_note-102] *Red* was Swift's first number-one album in the UK.[^cite_note-103] During the promotion of *Red*, Swift was romantically involved with the political heir [Conor Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_family), and subsequently the singer [Harry Styles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Styles).[^cite_note-Billboard-2014-77]

Taylor Swift wearing a white top and a black fedora hat, smiling with red lips and curly hair

Swift on [the Red Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Red_Tour) in 2013

The two most successful singles from *Red*, "[We Are Never Ever Getting Back Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Are_Never_Ever_Getting_Back_Together)" and "[I Knew You Were Trouble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Knew_You_Were_Trouble)", peaked at numbers one and two on the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-104] Both songs also reached the top five on the [UK singles chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_singles_chart), and the former was Swift's first chart topper in the US.[^cite_note-Griffiths-2021-105][^cite_note-106] The singles "[Begin Again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Begin_Again_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" and "[Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" peaked in the top 10 of the *Billboard* Hot 100,[^cite_note-Billboard-3-66] while "[Everything Has Changed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everything_Has_Changed)" and "[22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/22_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" reached the top 10 on the UK singles chart.[^cite_note-Griffiths-2021-105] [The Red Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Red_Tour) ran from March 2013 to June 2014 and became the highest-grossing country tour with revenue of \$150.2 million upon completion.[^cite_note-107] Swift was named Artist of the Year at the American Music Awards in 2013.[^cite_note-108]

Swift wrote and recorded two songs for [the soundtrack album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunger_Games:_Songs_from_District_12_and_Beyond) to the 2012 dystopian film *[The Hunger Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunger_Games_\(film\))*: "[Eyes Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyes_Open_\(song\))" and "[Safe & Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_&_Sound_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))".[^cite_note-109] The latter, co-written with [the Civil Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Civil_Wars) and [T-Bone Burnett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-Bone_Burnett), won the [Grammy Award for Best Song Written for Visual Media](415037.md) in 2013.[^cite_note-110] She wrote and recorded "[Sweeter than Fiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweeter_than_Fiction)" for the soundtrack to the 2013 biographical film *[One Chance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Chance_\(film\))*,[^cite_note-111] and featured as a guest vocalist on [B.o.B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B.o.B)'s 2012 single "[Both of Us](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Both_of_Us)" and Tim McGraw's 2013 single "[Highway Don't Care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highway_Don't_Care)".[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] Her acting roles included a voice acting role in the 2012 animated film *[The Lorax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lorax_\(film\))*[^cite_note-114] and a supporting role in the 2014 dystopian film [*The Giver*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Giver_\(film\)).[^cite_note-115]

### 2014–2018: *1989* and *Reputation*

Swift relocated from Nashville to New York City in March 2014 and transformed her image from country to [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music) with her fifth album, *[1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_\(album\))*.[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117] She produced *1989* with Martin, Shellback, Chapman, and new collaborators [Jack Antonoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Antonoff), [Imogen Heap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imogen_Heap), [Ryan Tedder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Tedder), and [Ali Payami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Payami).[^cite_note-118] Released on October 27, 2014, the album spent eleven weeks at number one and one year in the top 10 of the *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] It has sold 14 million copies worldwide, becoming Swift's best-selling album.[^cite_note-Winter-2022-121] Three of *1989*'s singles—"[Shake It Off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shake_It_Off)", "[Blank Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blank_Space)", and "[Bad Blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Blood_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))"—reached number one on the *Billboard* Hot 100; the first two made Swift [the first woman to replace herself at the top spot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Billboard_Hot_100_chart_achievements_and_milestones).[^cite_note-122] Two other singles—"[Style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Style_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" and "[Wildest Dreams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildest_Dreams)"—peaked at numbers six and five, making *1989* the first album by Swift to have five consecutive top 10 singles on the Hot 100.[^cite_note-123]

Taylor Swift performing with a mic, wearing a white strapless top, white shorts, and black boots, with fabric connecting her shorts to her boots

Swift on [the 1989 World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_1989_World_Tour), the highest-grossing tour of 2015

Swift supported *1989* with [the 1989 World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_1989_World_Tour), the [highest-grossing tour of 2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours#Highest-grossing_tours_by_year) with \$250 million in revenue.[^cite_note-124] She was named *Billboard*'s Woman of the Year and received the inaugural [Dick Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Clark) Award for Excellence at the American Music Awards in 2014,[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] and "Bad Blood" won [Video of the Year](476903.md) and [Best Collaboration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Collaboration) at the MTV Video Music Awards in 2015.[^cite_note-127] At the [58th Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/58th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) in 2016, *1989* made Swift the first woman to win Album of the Year twice and also won [Best Pop Vocal Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Pop_Vocal_Album), while "Bad Blood" won [Best Music Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Music_Video).[^cite_note-128] The most-awarded pop album of all time,[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] *1989* was selected for preservation in the [National Recording Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Recording_Registry) in 2026.[^cite_note-131]

During the promotion of *1989*, Swift publicly opposed free [music streaming services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_streaming_service) and their compensation of artists. She published an [op-ed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Op-ed) in *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* in July 2014 to stress the importance of albums as a creative medium for artists,[^cite_note-132] and, in November, removed her discography from ad-supported, free streaming platforms such as [Spotify](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spotify).[^cite_note-133] Big Machine kept her music only on paid, subscription-required platforms.[^cite_note-Christman-2014-134] In a June 2015 open letter, Swift criticized [Apple Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Music) for not offering royalties to artists during its free three-month trial period and threatened to withdraw her music from the platform,[^cite_note-135] prompting [Apple Inc.](856.md) to announce that it would pay artists during the free trial period.[^cite_note-136] Big Machine returned Swift's catalog to Spotify and other free streaming platforms in June 2017.[^cite_note-137]

Swift dated the DJ [Calvin Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvin_Harris) from March 2015 to June 2016.[^cite_note-138] They co-wrote "[This Is What You Came For](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_Is_What_You_Came_For)", an [EDM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_dance_music) single by Harris featuring [Rihanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rihanna); Swift was initially credited under the pseudonym Nils Sjöberg.[^cite_note-139] Swift also wrote "[Better Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Better_Man_\(Little_Big_Town_song\))", recorded by [Little Big Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Big_Town) in 2016, which won the Country Music Association Award for Song of the Year.[^cite_note-Ryan-2017-140] She wrote and recorded "[I Don't Wanna Live Forever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Don't_Wanna_Live_Forever)" with [Zayn Malik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zayn_Malik) for [the soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifty_Shades_Darker:_Original_Motion_Picture_Soundtrack) to the 2017 film *[Fifty Shades Darker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifty_Shades_Darker_\(film\))*; it peaked at number two on the *Billboard* Hot 100, the highest for a [*Fifty Shades*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifty_Shades_\(film_series\)) single.[^cite_note-141]

Taylor Swift wearing a sparkling snake-patterned bodysuit while performing onstage

Swift on her [Reputation Stadium Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_Stadium_Tour) in 2018

In April 2016, Kanye West released the single "[Famous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famous_\(Kanye_West_song\))", in which he references Swift in the line, "I made that bitch famous." Swift criticized West and said she never consented to the lyric, but West claimed that he had received her approval, and his then-wife, [Kim Kardashian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Kardashian), released video clips of Swift and West discussing the song amicably over the phone. Although the clips were proven to be purposefully edited,[^cite_note-142] the controversy made Swift a subject of an online "[cancel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cancel_culture)" movement, in which her critics denounced her as a fake and calculating "snake".[^cite_note-143] In late 2016, after briefly dating the actor [Tom Hiddleston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Hiddleston), Swift began a six-year relationship with the actor [Joe Alwyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Alwyn) and went on hiatus.[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2017444-145]

In August 2017, Swift [countersued and won a case against David Mueller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_sexual_assault_trial), a former [radio jockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_jockey), who sued her for damages from loss of employment. Four years earlier, she informed Mueller's employer that he had sexually assaulted her by groping her at an event.[^cite_note-146] Public controversies influenced Swift's sixth album, *[Reputation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_\(album\))*, which explores themes of fame and finding love amidst the tumultuous affairs.[^cite_note-147] Released on November 10, 2017,[^cite_note-148] *Reputation* debuted atop the *Billboard* 200 with 1.21 million US sales and recorded first-week worldwide sales of two million copies.[^cite_note-149]

*Reputation*'s lead single, "[Look What You Made Me Do](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do)", topped the *Billboard* Hot 100 with the highest sales and streaming week of 2017,[^cite_note-150] and was Swift's first UK number-one single.[^cite_note-151] The singles "[...Ready for It?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/...Ready_for_It%3F)", "[End Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_Game_\(song\))", and "[Delicate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delicate_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" were released to pop radio and all peaked in the top 20 of the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-152] In 2018, Swift featured on [Sugarland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarland)'s "[Babe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babe_\(Sugarland_song\))" and embarked on the [Reputation Stadium Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_Stadium_Tour), which grossed \$345.7 million worldwide.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154]

### 2018–2021: *Lover*, *Folklore*, and *Evermore*

In November 2018, Swift signed a record deal with [Universal Music Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Music_Group), which promoted her albums under [Republic Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Records)' imprint.[^cite_note-155] The contract included a provision allowing Swift to maintain ownership of her [master recordings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_recording). In addition, if Universal sold any part of its stake in Spotify, it agreed to distribute a non-recoupable portion of the proceeds to its artists.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157]

A portrait of Swift

Swift at the [American Music Awards of 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Music_Awards_of_2019), where she was named Artist of the Decade

Swift's first album with Republic Records and seventh overall, *[Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_\(album\))*, was released on August 23, 2019.[^cite_note-158] She produced the album with Antonoff, [Louis Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Bell), [Frank Dukes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Dukes), and [Joel Little](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel_Little).[^cite_note-Catucci-2019-159] *Lover* peaked atop the charts of such countries as Australia, Canada, Ireland, Mexico, Norway, Sweden, the UK, and the US,[^cite_note-160] and was the global best-selling album by a solo artist of 2019.[^cite_note-161] Three of its singles—"[Me!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me!)", "[You Need to Calm Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Need_to_Calm_Down)", and "[Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))"—were released in 2019 and peaked in the top 10 of the *Billboard* Hot 100. "[The Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Man_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" was released in 2020 and charted highly, and "[Cruel Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" became a resurgent success in 2023, reaching number one.[^cite_note-162] Swift promoted *Lover* with the one-off [City of Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Lover) concert, [its accompanying television special](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Lover#Video_and_music_releases), and the live album *[Lover (Live from Paris)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_\(Live_from_Paris\))*, which set the record for the largest vinyl sales week by a live album in the US.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-bb2025-164]

In 2019, Swift was named Artist of the Decade by the American Music Awards and Woman of the Decade by *Billboard*,[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166] and became the first female artist to win the MTV Video Music Award for Video of the Year for a self-directed video, with "You Need to Calm Down".[^cite_note-167] During the promotion of *Lover*, Swift became embroiled in [a public dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_masters_dispute) with the talent manager [Scooter Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scooter_Braun) after he purchased Big Machine Records, including the masters of her albums under the label.[^cite_note-Grady-2019-168] Swift said that Big Machine would allow her to acquire the masters only if she exchanged one new album for each older one under a new contract, which she declined.[^cite_note-Grady-2019-168] In November 2020, Swift began [re-recording](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-recording_\(music\)) her back catalog to control the licensing of her songs for [commercial use](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchronization_rights).[^cite_note-169]

In February 2020, Swift signed a global publishing deal with [Universal Music Publishing Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Music_Publishing_Group) after her 16-year contract with Sony/ATV expired.[^cite_note-170] During the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), Swift collaborated with Antonoff and [Aaron Dessner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Dessner) on two "sister albums" [surprise-released](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surprise_album) in 2020: *[Folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_\(Taylor_Swift_album\))* on July 24, and *[Evermore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evermore)* on December 11.[^cite_note-171] Joe Alwyn co-wrote and co-produced several songs under the pseudonym William Bowery.[^cite_note-172] Both albums incorporate atmospheric [indie folk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indie_folk) and [indie rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indie_rock) sounds with [orchestrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchestration);[^cite_note-Doyle-2021-173][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGrath202379FogartyArnold20215-174] each had three singles sent to US pop, country, and [adult alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_album_alternative) radio—"[Cardigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardigan_\(song\))", "[Betty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betty_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", and "[Exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exile_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" from *Folklore*, and "[Willow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_\(song\))", "[No Body, No Crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_Body,_No_Crime)", and "[Coney Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coney_Island_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" from *Evermore*.[^cite_note-175] *Folklore* and "Cardigan" made Swift the first artist to debut a number-one album and a number-one song in the same week in the US; she achieved the feat again with *Evermore* and "Willow".[^cite_note-176] Swift promoted *Folklore* with the documentary concert film *[Folklore: The Long Pond Studio Sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore:_The_Long_Pond_Studio_Sessions)* (2020);[^cite_note-177] [its accompanying live album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore:_The_Long_Pond_Studio_Sessions_\(From_the_Disney+_Special\)) became the first [Record Store Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Record_Store_Day)-exclusive album to reach the top 10 of the *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-178]

Swift won Artist of the Year at the American Music Awards in 2020[^cite_note-179] and Album of the Year for *Folklore* at the [63rd Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/63rd_Annual_Grammy_Awards) in 2021, becoming the first woman to win the Grammy Award for Album of the Year three times.[^cite_note-180] She played [Bombalurina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombalurina) in the musical fantasy film *[Cats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cats_\(2019_film\))* (2019), for which she co-wrote and recorded the original song "[Beautiful Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beautiful_Ghosts)".[^cite_note-181] The documentary *[Miss Americana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miss_Americana)*, which chronicled parts of Swift's life and career, premiered at the [2020 Sundance Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Sundance_Film_Festival) and received positive reviews.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183]

### 2021–2023: Re-recordings and *Midnights*

Taylor Swift singing into a mic, dressed in a two-piece leather outfit

Swift performing in 2022

Swift released two re-recorded albums in 2021: *[Fearless (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_\(Taylor's_Version\))* on April 9, and *[Red (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_\(Taylor's_Version\))* on November 12. Both peaked atop the *Billboard* 200; the former was the first re-recorded album to do so.[^cite_note-184] The latter helped Swift become the female musician with the most weeks at number one on the [Top Country Albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top_Country_Albums) chart, and its song "[All Too Well (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Too_Well_\(Taylor's_Version\))" became the longest song to top the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186]

Swift's tenth album, *[Midnights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnights)*, was released on October 21, 2022.[^cite_note-The_Guardian-2022-187] *Midnights* was Swift's fifth album to open atop the *Billboard* 200 chart with US first-week sales of one million. Its tracks, led by the single "[Anti-Hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Hero_\(song\))", made her the first artist to occupy the entire top 10 of the *Billboard* Hot 100 the same week.[^cite_note-Dailey-2022-188] The album peaked atop the charts of at least 14 other countries, including Australia, Canada, France, Germany, Italy, Norway, and Sweden.[^cite_note-189] Two other singles, "[Lavender Haze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lavender_Haze)" and "[Karma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karma_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))", both peaked at number two on the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-190]

In 2023, Swift released two re-recorded albums: *[Speak Now (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_\(Taylor's_Version\))* on July 7, and *[1989 (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_\(Taylor's_Version\))* on October 27. Both debuted atop the *Billboard* 200, and the former made Swift the female artist with the most number-one albums on the chart.[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192] The single "[Is It Over Now?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Is_It_Over_Now%3F)" from *1989 (Taylor's Version)* reached number one on the *Billboard* Hot 100, making Swift the first woman to have three number-one songs from three different albums in a calendar year.[^cite_note-193] Swift featured on [Big Red Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Red_Machine_\(band\))'s "[Renegade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renegade_\(Big_Red_Machine_song\))" and "Birch" (2021),[^cite_note-194] [Haim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haim_\(band\))'s "[Gasoline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gasoline_\(Haim_song\))" (2021),[^cite_note-195] [Ed Sheeran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Sheeran)'s "[The Joker and the Queen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Joker_and_the_Queen)" (2022),[^cite_note-196] and [the National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_National_\(band\))'s "[The Alcott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Alcott)" (2023).[^cite_note-197] She wrote and recorded "[Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina_\(Taylor_Swift_song\))" for [the soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Where_the_Crawdads_Sing_\(soundtrack\)) to the 2022 mystery film *[Where the Crawdads Sing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Where_the_Crawdads_Sing_\(film\))*,[^cite_note-198] and had a supporting role in the 2022 period comedy film *[Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam_\(2022_film\))*.[^cite_note-199] After splitting from Alwyn in April 2023, Swift dated the musician [Matty Healy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matty_Healy) in May–June.[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201]

In 2022, Swift won Artist of the Year at the American Music Awards[^cite_note-202] and Video of the Year for *[All Too Well: The Short Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Too_Well:_The_Short_Film)*, her self-directed short film that accompanies "All Too Well (10 Minute Version)", at the MTV Video Music Awards; *All Too Well* also won the Grammy Award for Best Music Video.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204] The following year, she again won the MTV Video Music Award for Video of the Year with "Anti-Hero",[^cite_note-205] became the first musician to rank at number one on *Billboard*'s year-end top artists list in three different decades (2009, 2015 and 2023),[^cite_note-206] and had five out of the 10 best-selling albums of the year in the US, a record since [Luminate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luminate_\(company\)) began tracking US music sales in 1991.[^cite_note-207] At the [66th Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/66th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) in 2024, *Midnights* made Swift the first artist to win Album of the Year four times; it also won Best Pop Vocal Album.[^cite_note-208]

### 2023–present: The Eras Tour, *The Tortured Poets Department*, and *The Life of a Showgirl*

Taylor Swift singing into a mic, wearing a fringed dress

Swift on [the Eras Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Eras_Tour) in 2023

In March 2023, Swift embarked on [the Eras Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Eras_Tour), which she conceived as a tribute to her entire discography. The tour spanned five continents through December 2024.[^cite_note-209] It exerted [a global cultural, economic, and political impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_Eras_Tour) and culminated in an unprecedented height of popularity for Swift,[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] resulting in a phenomenon that the media dubbed "Swiftmania".[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] The Eras Tour became the first tour to gross \$1 billion and the [highest-grossing tour in history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours), with \$2 billion in revenue.[^cite_note-215] [Its 2023 concert film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Eras_Tour) became the highest-grossing of its kind with \$250 million,[^cite_note-216] and [its 2024 photobook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Eras_Tour_Book) sold nearly one million copies in its first week in the US,[^cite_note-217] followed by [a 2025 documentary miniseries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_End_of_an_Era) on [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+).[^cite_note-218] The events that happened during the tour inspired Swift's eleventh and twelfth albums, *[The Tortured Poets Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tortured_Poets_Department)* (2024) and *[The Life of a Showgirl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_a_Showgirl)* (2025).[^cite_note-Sisario-2024-219][^cite_note-Dunn-2025-220]

During the run of the Eras Tour, there were controversies surrounding [Ticketmaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ticketmaster)'s monopoly that led to [political scrutiny in the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift%E2%80%93Ticketmaster_controversy),[^cite_note-221] venue mismanagement that led to [a concertgoer's death in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Ana_Clara_Benevides),[^cite_note-222] and Singapore's exclusivity deal that led to political tension in Southeast Asia.[^cite_note-223] In January 2024, [AI-generated pornographic images portraying Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_deepfake_pornography_controversy) were posted to [Twitter](9988187.md) and spread to other social media platforms, spurring criticism and demands for legal reform.[^cite_note-224] In July 2024, [three children were killed in a stabbing attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Southport_stabbing) at a Swift-themed workshop in [Southport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southport), England, leading to [civil unrest in the UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Kingdom_riots).[^cite_note-225] The following month, the [Vienna](55866.md) concerts were canceled following the arrest of suspects who [planned a terrorist attack](77580851.md).[^cite_note-226] Swift began dating the football player [Travis Kelce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travis_Kelce) in August 2023,[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228] with the media dubbing them a "[supercouple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercouple)".[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] They became engaged in August 2025 and married on July 3, 2026, in [a highly publicized private ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wedding_of_Taylor_Swift_and_Travis_Kelce) at [Madison Square Garden](75129.md) in New York City.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

*The Tortured Poets Department* was released on April 19, 2024. The same day, it was expanded into a [double album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_album), subtitled *The Anthology*.[^cite_note-233] It became the first album to surpass one billion streams on Spotify in a single week and accumulated 4 million units worldwide within its first release week.[^cite_note-Dailey-2024-234][^cite_note-235] In the US, *The Tortured Poets Department* debuted atop the *Billboard* 200 with 2.6 million first-week units and stayed at number one for seventeen weeks, becoming Swift's longest-running number-one album.[^cite_note-236] It was the global best-selling album of 2024, with 5.6 million pure copies sold.[^cite_note-237] Its songs made Swift the first artist to monopolize the top 14 positions of the *Billboard* Hot 100 in the same week;[^cite_note-Dailey-2024-234] the lead single, "[Fortnight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortnight_\(song\))", reached number one, while the second single, "[I Can Do It with a Broken Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Can_Do_It_with_a_Broken_Heart)", peaked at number three.[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] In May 2025, Swift finalized the purchase of the masters to her first six original albums from [Shamrock Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamrock_Holdings), which had acquired them from Braun in 2020.[^cite_note-240]

*The Life of a Showgirl* was released on October 3, 2025,[^cite_note-Dunn-2025-220] accompanied by the promotional film *[Taylor Swift: The Official Release Party of a Showgirl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Official_Release_Party_of_a_Showgirl)*, which grossed \$50 million worldwide.[^cite_note-241] The album debuted atop the *Billboard* 200 with 4 million first-week units, including 3.4 million pure sales, becoming the [fastest-selling album in US history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_fastest-selling_albums#United_States).[^cite_note-242] It was the global best-selling album of 2025, with 6.05 million pure copies sold.[^cite_note-243] Its tracks occupied the top 12 spots of the *Billboard* Hot 100,[^cite_note-BBOphelia-244] the Canadian Hot 100,[^cite_note-245] and the [Australian Singles Chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Singles_Chart).[^cite_note-246] The singles "[The Fate of Ophelia](81126617.md)" and "[Opalite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opalite_\(song\))" both reached number one in the US[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] and the UK,[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] while the third single, "[Elizabeth Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Taylor_\(song\))", peaked at number three on the Hot 100.[^cite_note-251] In 2026, Swift co-wrote and recorded "[I Knew It, I Knew You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Knew_It,_I_Knew_You)" for [the soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toy_Story_5_\(soundtrack\)) to the animated film *[Toy Story 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toy_Story_5)*; it debuted atop the *Billboard* Hot 100, becoming the first song from a [Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney) film to do so.[^cite_note-Trust-2026-252] She released the deluxe album *The Life of a Showgirl: The Encore* on September 25, 2026, containing four additional songs, including "[Patient Zero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patient_Zero_\(song\))".[^cite_note-253]

## Artistry

### Musical styles

With continuous musical reinventions,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranssen202290-254] Swift has been described as a "musical chameleon" by journalists and music analysts alike.[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESloanHarding201921-257] Her discography spans styles of country, pop, and [folk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music),[^cite_note-258] incorporating subgenres like [country pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_pop),[^cite_note-259] [pop rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_rock),[^cite_note-Petridis-2009-260] [synth-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synth-pop),[^cite_note-261] [country rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_rock), [electropop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electropop), and indie rock,[^cite_note-262] with elements of [rock](25423.md), [rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rap_music), [electronica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronica), [indie pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indie_pop), and [R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R\&B).[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-Powers-2022-264][^cite_note-Powers-2024-265][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone201746–49-266] Swift self-identified as a country musician with her first four albums,[^cite_note-Rosen-2013-267][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcNutt202077Gasser2019310-268] influenced by 1990s female country artists such as Shania Twain, Faith Hill, [LeAnn Rimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeAnn_Rimes), and the Dixie Chicks,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpencer201016–18-269] as well as Keith Urban's incorporation of rock, pop, and [blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues) into country music.[^cite_note-270] The albums feature a country pop sound defined by instruments such as the [six-string banjo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Six-string_banjo), [mandolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandolin), and [fiddle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiddle), a slight [twang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twang) in Swift's vocals, and pop rock melodies;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHughes2017206Perone201733Carlin2019115-271][^cite_note-272] *Speak Now* expanded this sound with 1970s and 1980s rock influences, including [pop-punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop-punk) and [arena rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arena_rock).[^cite_note-Caramanica-2010-88][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone201729,_42,_46,_49-273] Some critics argued that country signified Swift's songwriting more than her musical style,[^cite_note-Petridis-2009-260][^cite_note-Jones-2008-274] and accused her of causing mainstream country to stray from its roots.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHughes2017206-275][^cite_note-276]

*Red*'s incorporation of [eclectic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclecticism_in_music) pop and rock styles, including [Britrock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britrock), [dance-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance-pop), and [dubstep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubstep),[^cite_note-Mansfield-2012-101][^cite_note-277] led to a critical debate over Swift's status as a country musician.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone20172-278] Swift then chose 1980s synth-pop to recalibrate her artistic identity, inspired by the music of [Phil Collins](153557.md), [Annie Lennox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annie_Lennox), [Peter Gabriel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Gabriel), and [Madonna](142056.md).[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280] *1989*, the first album in this direction, incorporates [electronic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music) arrangements consisting of dense [synthesizers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthesizers) and [drum machines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_machine).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcNutt202079Gasser2019310-281] Swift expanded on the electronic production on her next albums.[^cite_note-Levine-2019-282] *Reputation* features a [trap-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trap-pop) production[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] with elements of hip-hop, R\&B, and EDM, and [maximalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximalism_\(music\)) arrangements of heavy [bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bass_\(sound\)) and manipulated vocals.[^cite_note-Powers-2019-285][^cite_note-Hyden-2017-286][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGasser2019310-287] *Lover* is a pop album[^cite_note-288] with a 1980s-influenced sound combining [dream pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dream_pop), synth-pop, and pop rock.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-Catucci-2019-159] *Midnights* and *The Tortured Poets Department* both have a [minimalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimal_music) synth-pop sound characterized by [analog synthesizers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Analog_synthesizers), sustained [bass notes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bass_note), and simple [drum machine patterns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_pattern),[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] although *The Tortured Poets Department* features a more understated production.[^cite_note-293] *The Life of a Showgirl* incorporates pop and [soft rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_rock), featuring [new wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_wave_music)-leaning compositions and prominent acoustic guitars.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDoyle20253-296] When Swift embraced a pop identity, [rockist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockist) critics regarded it as an erosion of her country songwriting authenticity,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcNutt202079Sloan202117Stone202360–61-297] while others considered it necessary for her artistic evolution and defended her as a pioneer of [poptimism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poptimism).[^cite_note-Winter-2022-121][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESloan202117-298]

Her 2020 albums *Folklore* and *Evermore*, described by some critics as "alternative",[^cite_note-Snapes-2022-299][^cite_note-300] explore indie folk and rock styles. They incorporate a subtle, stripped-back soundscape with orchestrations, muted synthesizers, and [drum pads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_pad).[^cite_note-Doyle-2021-173][^cite_note-Hyden-2021-301][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGrath202378–79-302] *Evermore* experiments with varied [song structures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_structure), asymmetric [time signatures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_signature), and diverse instruments.[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-Barna-2020-304] Critics deemed the indie styles a mature representation of Swift's singer-songwriter status and credited her with popularizing "alternative" music, although there was disagreement over this description.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGrath202378–79Hibbett202223-305]

### Voice

Swift possesses a [mezzo-soprano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezzo-soprano) singing voice,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFulford2014192-310] but she mostly sings in her [alto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alto) range.[^cite_note-Powers-2014-311][^cite_note-Lambert-2014-312][^cite_note-313] Her [pitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitch_\(music\)), [dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dialect), and [accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accent_\(sociolinguistics\)) have undergone many changes throughout her career.[^cite_note-314] Reviews of her early country albums criticized her vocals as weak and strained compared to those of other female country singers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEProvenzano2018173-315] Defenders of Swift appreciated that she refrained from correcting her pitch with [Auto-Tune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto-Tune) and that she prioritized intimacy and emotion to communicate the messages of her songs with her audience,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEProvenzano2018173–174Pecknold201681-316] a style that critics have described as conversational.[^cite_note-Powers-2014-311][^cite_note-317] According to the critic [Ann Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Powers), this singing style is demonstrated through Swift's attention to detail, which conveys an exact feeling—"the subtle adjustment of words and phrases to suggest moods like doubt, hope, and intimacy".[^cite_note-Powers-2014-311][^cite_note-318]

On *Red* and *1989*, Swift's vocals are processed with electronic effects, such as synthesizer tweaking, looping, and multitracking, to accompany the pop production.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone201746–48-319][^cite_note-Powers-2014-311] Her voice on *Reputation* and *Midnights* incorporates hip-hop and R\&B influences, resulting in a near-[rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rap) delivery that emphasizes rhythm and [cadence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadence) over melody.[^cite_note-Powers-2022-264][^cite_note-320] She uses her lower [register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Register_\(music\)) vocals extensively in *Folklore*[^cite_note-321] and both her lower and upper registers in *Evermore*; the [musicologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musicologist) Alyssa Barca described her [timbre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbre) in the upper register as "breathy and bright" and the lower register as "full and dark".[^cite_note-Barna-2020-304]

Swift's vocals have received more positive assessments since *Folklore*. The critic [Amanda Petrusich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanda_Petrusich) commented in 2023 that her singing had become richer, with greater clarity and a stronger tone, even in live performances.[^cite_note-Petrusich-2023-322] *Rolling Stone* ranked her 102nd on their 2023 list "[The 200 Greatest Singers of All Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_200_Greatest_Singers_of_All_Time)"; the magazine argued that her breathy timbre allows for a broad range of delivery and commented: "A decade ago, including her on this list would have been a controversial move, but recent releases like *Folklore*, *Evermore*, and *Midnights* officially settled the argument."[^cite_note-323] Powers attributed Swift's vocal versatility to her evolving artistry, citing her use of hip-hop cadences and country-influenced timbre.[^cite_note-Powers-2022-264]

### Songwriting

Swift is credited as the lead songwriter on every song she has released and as the sole songwriter on over 60 tracks.[^cite_note-324] Her fascination with songwriting began in her childhood; she credited her mother with igniting her early interest by helping her prepare for class presentations,[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] and she would make up lyrics to [Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney) soundtrack songs once she had run out of words while singing them.[^cite_note-327] Her early influences included the country musicians [Patsy Cline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patsy_Cline), [Loretta Lynn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loretta_Lynn), [Tammy Wynette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammy_Wynette), and Dolly Parton;[^cite_note-Rolling_Stone_Interview-19][^cite_note-Roland-2011-95] the singer-songwriters [Joni Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joni_Mitchell), [Melissa Etheridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melissa_Etheridge), [Sarah McLachlan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_McLachlan), and [Alanis Morissette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alanis_Morissette);[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329] and the [emo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emo) musicians [Pete Wentz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Wentz) of [Fall Out Boy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Out_Boy) and [Chris Carrabba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Carrabba) of [Dashboard Confessional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dashboard_Confessional).[^cite_note-Hiatt-2019-330][^cite_note-West-Songwriting-331] She credited songs by the Dixie Chicks, [Jeannie C. Riley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeannie_C._Riley), and [Kenny Chesney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenny_Chesney) with influencing her approach to narrative songwriting,[^cite_note-West-Songwriting-331] and listed [Paul McCartney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_McCartney), [Bruce Springsteen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen), [Emmylou Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmylou_Harris), and [Kris Kristofferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kris_Kristofferson) as career role models for their consistent and evolving songwriting.[^cite_note-Widdicombe-2011-28][^cite_note-332]

Swift considers herself a songwriter first and foremost.[^cite_note-Widdicombe-2011-28][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESanneh202171-333] She divides her lyrics into three types: "[quill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quill) lyrics", which feature antiquated words and phrasing; "[fountain pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fountain_pen) lyrics", which combine modern storylines with poetic twists and vivid imagery; and "glitter [gel pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gel_pen) lyrics", which are lively and frivolous.[^cite_note-334] Swift has used songwriting to cope with personal experiences; her songs are largely autobiographical and feature narratives that mostly revolve around love and romantic relationships.[^cite_note-Jo_Sales-2013-29][^cite_note-335][^cite_note-Faichney-2024-336] In her songwriting process, she would start by identifying an emotion she wanted to convey, and the story and melody would follow.[^cite_note-337] Swift has characterized her songwriting as "confessional",[^cite_note-Vanity_Fair-2013-338][^cite_note-339] and academics have linked her style to [confessional poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessional_poetry), noting that her songs reference personal events and express her internal feelings to her audience.[^cite_note-Faichney-2024-336][^cite_note-340]

Where *Taylor Swift* and *Fearless* are rooted in adolescent feelings and detail an optimistic romance inspired by fairy tales, *Speak Now* reflects Swift's young adulthood, offering newfound wisdom on real-life heartbreak.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone201721,_42-341][^cite_note-Caramanica-2010-88] *Speak Now* marked the beginning of increasingly intense media speculation about the subjects of her songs, particularly her dating history;[^cite_note-342] Swift has described such scrutiny as sexist.[^cite_note-Vanity_Fair-2013-338] *Red* explores the tumult of an intense breakup, and *1989* reflects on failed relationships with a wistful perspective; both incorporate lyrics that hint at sex, reflecting her personal growth.[^cite_note-Lambert-2014-312] *Reputation* addresses the public controversies that tarnished Swift's wholesome image while portraying a blossoming romance with intimacy and vulnerability; its extensive references to sex and alcohol marked a departure from the youthful innocence that had informed her past albums.[^cite_note-Powers-2019-285][^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] Swift described *Lover* as a "love letter to love", inspired by her realization of "love that was very real".[^cite_note-345]

On *Folklore* and *Evermore*, Swift was inspired by [escapism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escapism) and [romanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism) to explore fictional narratives.[^cite_note-346] She imposed her emotions onto imagined characters and story arcs, inspired by authors and poets of [romantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romantic_literature) and [modernist literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernist_literature) like [F. Scott Fitzgerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F._Scott_Fitzgerald), [Robert Frost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Frost), [William Wordsworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Wordsworth), and [Emily Dickinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Dickinson);[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJuko2024237,_240Smith202478–80Geary2024135-348] the last of whom was a distant cousin of Swift.[^cite_note-349] The albums' characters construct their narratives from fragments of memory, symbolizing the nature of folktales and oral traditions that endure over time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHurley2024182Juko2024240-350] Swift returned to autobiographical songwriting on *Midnights*, whose nocturnal ruminations encompass regrets and fantasies informed by her awareness of fame.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-Light-2022-352] *The Tortured Poets Department* explores heartbreak and other themes to extremes, including erotic desire, forbidden love, and escaping the public spotlight,[^cite_note-Powers-2024-265][^cite_note-353] while *The Life of a Showgirl* draws on Swift's experiences during the Eras Tour as informed by her fame and romantic life.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355]

Critical reception of Swift's songwriting has been largely positive,[^cite_note-Widdicombe-2011-28][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-Lansky-2023-357] and her melodic compositions have been highlighted for optimizing the [verse-chorus form](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verse-chorus_form) with memorable [bridges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridge_\(music\)).[^cite_note-Powers-2014-311][^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] Several scholars have credited her with taking the confessional singer-songwriter tradition to new heights,[^cite_note-Sanders-2022-360][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESemler202314-361] and journalists have variously described her as a "[poet laureate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poet_laureate)"—of puberty,[^cite_note-Widdicombe-2011-28][^cite_note-362] of romance,[^cite_note-363] and of her generation.[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365] In the view of the literary critic [Stephanie Burt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephanie_Burt), Swift's writing is proficient at "placing inventive, evocative language into pop melodies designed to be sung".[^cite_note-366] Some critics have dismissed her "confessional" style as material for [tabloid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabloid_journalism) gossip.[^cite_note-367] Objection to the perceived poetic value of her songs, mostly from rockist critics, views her as a pop star using literary subtexts as a commercial ploy, with metaphors that are at times imprecise or self-indulgent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHibbett202224–26-368] Scholars have attributed such criticisms to sexism. The musicologist Travis Stimeling argued that while Swift's autobiographical authenticity fits country and rock standards, her mostly male detractors view her depictions of young women's experiences as trivial and unworthy of serious merit.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStimeling201690–91-369] According to the English-language academic Ryan Hibbett, this gendered criticism bars Swift from receiving the artistic credentials granted to figures such as [Bob Dylan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Dylan), whose romantic themes and occasional literary imprecisions are not as harshly criticized.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHibbett202225-370]

### Performances and stage

Swift in a white blouse playing a banjitar

Swift singing while playing a piano

Swift playing a [six-string banjo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Six-string_banjo) on the [Speak Now World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_World_Tour) (left, pictured in 2012) and a piano on the [Reputation Stadium Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_Stadium_Tour) (right, 2018)

Swift's concerts feature elaborate settings that incorporate elements of [Broadway theatre](725252.md) and [high tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_tech).[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373] She does not rely on elaborate choreography and instead emphasizes connecting emotionally with her audience through storytelling and vocal delivery.[^cite_note-374] Swift has used special guests and surprise songs or covers as recurring elements of her concerts;[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] the acoustic "surprise song" segment, which originated during the Speak Now World Tour, has become a fan favorite and one of the most anticipated parts of her shows.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Since 2007, she has toured with the same live band.[^cite_note-380] She plays four instruments live: guitar (including [electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_guitar), acoustic six-string, and [twelve-string](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-string_guitar)), six-string banjo, piano, and [ukulele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukulele).[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383]

Critics have praised her stage presence, stamina,[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] and ability to bring forth an intimate atmosphere for her audience, even in stadium settings.[^cite_note-Petrusich-2023-322][^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] The critic [Sasha Frere-Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasha_Frere-Jones) hailed her in 2008 as a "preternaturally skilled" entertainer who exerted professionalism with a vibrant energy.[^cite_note-Jones-2008-274] The journalist [Sam Lansky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Lansky) wrote in 2023: "Swift is many things onstage—vulnerable and triumphant, playful and sad—but the intimacy of her songcraft is front and center."[^cite_note-Lansky-2023-357]

### Videos and filmmaking

Swift emphasizes visuals as a key creative component of her music.[^cite_note-Ordoña-2022-388] She established her production company, [Taylor Swift Productions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_Productions), in 2008.[^cite_note-389] Her directorial debut was the music video for "Mine", co-directed with [Roman White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_White), in 2010;[^cite_note-390] and she developed the concept and [treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_treatment) for "Mean" in 2011.[^cite_note-391] For the music videos of the *1989* and *Reputation* singles, she collaborated extensively with the director [Joseph Kahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Kahn_\(director\)) on eight videos;[^cite_note-392] among them, she produced "Bad Blood", which won [Best Music Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Music_Video) at the Grammy Awards in 2016.[^cite_note-393] She worked with [American Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Express) for the "Blank Space" music video (which Kahn directed) and served as executive producer for the interactive app *AMEX Unstaged: Taylor Swift Experience*, for which she won a [Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Interactive Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award_for_Outstanding_Interactive_Program) in 2015.[^cite_note-394]

Swift has [directed 16 of her music videos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_videography).[^cite_note-395] "The Man" was her first solo directorial work and made her the first female artist to win the [MTV Video Music Award for Best Direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Direction).[^cite_note-396] *All Too Well: The Short Film*, her filmmaking debut, made her the first artist to win the Grammy Award for Best Music Video as a solo director.[^cite_note-Dailey-2022-188][^cite_note-397] In June 2023, Swift was invited to the [Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Motion_Picture_Arts_and_Sciences).[^cite_note-398] She has cited Joseph Kahn, [Chloé Zhao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chlo%C3%A9_Zhao), [Greta Gerwig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greta_Gerwig), [Nora Ephron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nora_Ephron), [Guillermo del Toro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillermo_del_Toro), [John Cassavetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Cassavetes), and [Noah Baumbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noah_Baumbach) as filmmaking influences.[^cite_note-Ordoña-2022-388][^cite_note-399]

## Achievements

Further information: [List of awards and nominations received by Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Taylor_Swift)

Side profile of Taylor Swift, wearing a red dress, with curly hair, holding a silver trophy

Swift with her [Best Female Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Female_Video) trophy at the [2009 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_MTV_Video_Music_Awards)
